[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this section.

  [[authorized person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[authorized person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      engaged or employed, or who was formerly engaged or employed, by
      or on behalf of Her Majesty in right of Canada to assist in
      carrying out the provisions of this Part. ([personne
      autorisée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the number (other
      than a Social Insurance Number) used by the Minister to identify a
      person registered for the purposes of this Part. ([numéro
      d'entreprise]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[confidential information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[confidential information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means information
      of any kind and in any form that relates to one or more persons
      and that is

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtained by or on behalf of the Minister for
        the purposes of this Act, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prepared from information referred to in
        paragraph (a),

      but does not include information that does not directly or
      indirectly reveal the identity of the person to whom it relates
      and, for the purposes of applying subsections (3), (13) and (15)
      to a representative of a government entity that is not an
      official, includes only the information described in paragraph
      211(6)(j) of the [Excise Act,
      2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1). ([renseignement
      confidentiel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[court of appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[court of appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([cour
      d'appel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[government entity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[government entity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a department or agency of the government of
        Canada or of a province;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a municipality;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an [[aboriginal
        government]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1)
        of the [Federal-Provincial Fiscal Arrangements
        Act](/eng/acts/F-7.8);

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a corporation all of the shares (except
        directors' qualifying shares) of the capital stock of which are
        owned by one or more persons each of which is

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province,

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a municipality, or

        - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a corporation described in this paragraph;
          or

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a board or commission, established by Her
        Majesty in right of Canada or a province, that performs an
        administrative or regulatory function of government, or by a
        municipality, that performs an administrative or regulatory
        function of a municipality. ([entité
        gouvernementale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[municipality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[municipality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an incorporated city,
      town, village, metropolitan authority, township, district, county
      or rural municipality or other incorporated municipal body however
      designated. ([municipalité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[official]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[official]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person that is employed
      in the service of, that occupies a position of responsibility in
      the service of, or that is engaged by or on behalf of Her Majesty
      in right of Canada or a province, or a person that was formerly so
      employed, that formerly occupied such a position or that formerly
      was so engaged. ([fonctionnaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} of a government entity
      means a person that is employed in the service of, that occupies a
      position of responsibility in the service of, or that is engaged
      by or on behalf of, a government entity, and includes, for the
      purposes of subsections (2), (3), (13) and (15), a person that was
      formerly so employed, that formerly occupied such a position or
      that formerly was so engaged. ([représentant]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of confidential information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as authorized under this section, an official
  or other representative of a government entity must not knowingly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide, or allow to be provided, to any person any
    confidential information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow any person to have access to any confidential
    information; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} use any confidential information other than in the
    course of the administration or enforcement of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential information evidence not
  compellable

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament or other law, no
  official or other representative of a government entity is required,
  in connection with any legal proceedings, to give or produce evidence
  relating to any confidential information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communications --- proceedings have
  been commenced

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) do not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} criminal proceedings, either by indictment or on
    summary conviction, that have been commenced by the laying of an
    information or the preferring of an indictment, under an Act of
    Parliament; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any legal proceedings relating to the
    administration or enforcement of this Act, the [Canada Pension
    Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), the [Employment Insurance
    Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) or any other Act of Parliament or law of a
    province that provides for the payment of a duty or tax.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized provision of confidential
  information

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may provide appropriate persons with any
  confidential information that may reasonably be regarded as necessary
  solely for a purpose relating to the life, health or safety of an
  individual or to the environment in Canada or any other country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of confidential information
  --- [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 211(6) and (6.1) of the [Excise Act,
  2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) apply in this Part, with any modifications
  that the circumstances require, as if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the references in those subsections to the [Excise
    Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) were references to this Part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} confidential information for the purposes of this
    Part were confidential information for the purposes of the [Excise
    Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an authorized person for the purposes of this Part
    were an authorized person for the purposes of the [Excise Act,
    2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a business number for the purposes of this Part
    were a business number for the purposes of the [Excise Act,
    2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of confidential information

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An official may provide

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any confidential information to an official of the
    Department of the Environment solely for the purposes of Part 2 or
    the formulation or evaluation of greenhouse gas pollution pricing
    policy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any confidential information relating to His
    Majesty in right of a province to an official of a department or
    agency of the Government of Canada, if His Majesty in right of the
    province or an agent of His Majesty in right of the province is not
    in compliance with this Part or has stated that it will not comply
    with this Part, solely for the purposes of the evaluation of, or the
    formulation of a response to, the non-compliance or statement; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any confidential information relating to an agent
    of His Majesty in right of a province to an official of a department
    or agency of the Government of Canada, if the agent or His Majesty
    in right of the province is not in compliance with this Part or has
    stated that it will not comply with this Part, solely for the
    purposes of the evaluation of, or the formulation of a response to,
    the non-compliance or statement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public disclosure

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in connection with a program,
  activity or service provided or undertaken by the Minister, make
  available to the public the business number of, and the name of
  (including any trade name or other name used by), the holder of a
  business number.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public disclosure by representative of
  government entity

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A representative of a government entity may, in
  connection with a program, activity or service provided or undertaken
  by the government entity, make available to the public the business
  number of, and the name of (including any trade name or other name
  used by), the holder of a business number, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a representative of the government entity was
    provided with that information under subsection (6); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the government entity uses the business number as
    an identifier in connection with the program, activity or service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public disclosure --- province or agent
  non-compliance

  [(9.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may communicate or otherwise make
  available to the public, in any manner that the Minister considers
  appropriate, confidential information relating to a person that is His
  Majesty in right of a province or an agent of His Majesty in right of
  a province if the following conditions are satisfied:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the person is His Majesty in right of a
    province, it is the case that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person is not in compliance with this Part or
      has publicly stated that it will not comply with this Part, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an agent of His Majesty in right of the province
      is not in compliance with this Part or has publicly stated that it
      will not comply with this Part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person is an agent of His Majesty in right
    of a province, it is the case that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person is not in compliance with this Part or
      has publicly stated that it will not comply with this Part, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} His Majesty in right of the province is not in
      compliance with this Part or has publicly stated that it will not
      comply with this Part; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the confidential information relates to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a registration status of, or the status of an
      application for registration by, the person under this Part,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a return that is filed, or required to be filed,
      by the person under this Part, including information that is
      contained in the return or that could reasonably be expected to be
      contained in the return,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is payable under this Part by or
      to the person, including a charge, rebate, net charge, penalty or
      amount of interest,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a projection or estimation of an amount that is,
      or that could reasonably be expected to be, payable under this
      Part by or to the person, including a charge, rebate, net charge,
      penalty or amount of interest,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is assessed under this Part in
      relation to the person,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which an amount referred to in any
      of subparagraphs (iii) to (v) has or has not been paid,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is paid under this Part by or to
      the person,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} a quantity of fuel or combustible waste that
      is delivered, imported, brought into a listed province, used or
      otherwise quantified for purposes of this Part,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} a projection or estimation of a quantity of fuel
      or combustible waste that could reasonably be expected to be
      delivered, imported, brought into a listed province, used or
      otherwise quantified for purposes of this Part,

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} a step that is, or is not, taken or contemplated
      by the Minister in the administration or enforcement of this Part
      relating to the person, including information relating to the
      collection of an amount, an assessment, an objection to an
      assessment, an audit or an appeal or legal proceeding,

    - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} a step that is or is not taken by the person in
      relation to its compliance or non-compliance with this Part,
      including information relating to the collection of an amount, an
      assessment, an objection to an assessment, an audit or an appeal
      or legal proceeding, or

    - [(xii)]{.lawlabel} a declaration or representation made by the
      person relating to anything set out in subparagraphs (i) to (xi).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}His Majesty --- information not
  confidential

  [(9.2)]{.lawlabel} Information relating to His Majesty in right of a
  province or an agent of His Majesty in right of a province that is
  communicated, or otherwise made available to the public, by the
  Minister under subsection (9.1) is deemed not to be confidential
  information for the purposes of this section and section 134.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious offences

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An official may provide information to a law
  enforcement officer of an appropriate police organization in the
  circumstances described in subsection 211(6.4) of the [Excise Act,
  2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats to security

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} An official may provide information to the head, or
  their delegate, of a recipient Government of Canada institution listed
  in Schedule 3 to the [Security of Canada Information Sharing
  Act](/eng/acts/S-6.9) in the circumstances described in subsection
  211(6.5) of the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to prevent unauthorized use or
  disclosure

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} The person presiding at a legal proceeding relating
  to the supervision, evaluation or discipline of an authorized person
  may order any measures that are necessary to ensure that confidential
  information is not used or provided to any person for any purpose not
  relating to that proceeding, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holding a hearing [in camera]{.otherLang
    lang="la"};

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} banning the publication of the information;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} concealing the identity of the person to whom the
    information relates; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sealing the records of the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure to person or on consent

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} An official or other representative of a government
  entity may provide confidential information relating to a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to that person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the consent of that person, to any other
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation of registration and
  business number

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} On being provided by any person with information
  specified by the Minister sufficient to identify a single person and a
  number, an official may confirm or deny that the following statements
  are both true:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the identified person is registered under Division
    4 of this Part; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number is the business number of the identified
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal from order or direction

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} An order or direction that is made in the course of
  or in connection with any legal proceedings and that requires an
  official or other representative of a government entity to give or
  produce evidence relating to any confidential information may, by
  notice served on all interested parties, be appealed forthwith by the
  Minister or by the person against whom the order or direction is made
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court of appeal of the province in which the
    order or direction is made, in the case of an order or direction
    made by a court or other tribunal established under the laws of the
    province, whether that court or tribunal is exercising a
    jurisdiction conferred by the laws of Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court of Appeal, in the case of an
    order or direction made by a court or other tribunal established
    under the laws of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} The court to which an appeal is taken under
  subsection (15) may allow the appeal and quash the order or direction
  appealed from or may dismiss the appeal, and the rules of practice and
  procedure from time to time governing appeals to the courts must
  apply, with any modifications that the circumstances require, in
  respect of an appeal instituted under subsection (15).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} An appeal instituted under subsection (15) must stay
  the operation of the order or direction appealed from until judgment
  is pronounced.

#### [SUBDIVISION F]{.HLabel3}[Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245510 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may assess a person for any charge or other amount payable by
  the person under this Part and may, despite any previous assessment
  covering, in whole or in part, the same matter, vary the assessment,
  reassess the person assessed or make any additional assessments that
  the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability not affected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The liability of a person to pay an amount under this
  Part is not affected by an incorrect or incomplete assessment or by
  the fact that no assessment has been made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister not bound

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister is not bound by any return, application
  or information provided by or on behalf of any person and may make an
  assessment despite any return, application or information provided or
  not provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rebate on reassessment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person has paid an amount assessed under this
  section and the amount paid exceeds the amount determined on
  reassessment to have been payable by the person, the Minister must
  provide a rebate to the person equal to the excess and, for the
  purpose of section 98, the rebate is deemed to have been required to
  be paid on the day on which the amount was paid to the Minister
  together with interest on the excess at the prescribed rate for the
  period beginning on the day the amount was paid by the person and
  ending on the day the rebate is paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of rebates

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In making an assessment, the Minister may take into
  account any rebate payable to the person being assessed under this
  Part. If the Minister does so, the person is deemed to have applied
  for the rebate under this Part on the day the notice of assessment is
  sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest on cancelled amounts

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (4), if a person has paid an
  amount of interest or penalty and the Minister waives or cancels that
  amount under section 100 or 125, the Minister must rebate the amount
  to the person, together with interest on the amount at the prescribed
  rate for the period beginning on the day that is 30 days after the day
  on which the Minister received a request in a manner satisfactory to
  the Minister to apply that section and ending on the day on which the
  rebate is paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on rebates

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An amount under this section must not be rebated to a
  person at any time unless all returns of which the Minister has
  knowledge and that are required to be filed at or before that time by
  the person under this Part, the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15), the
  [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), the [Air Travellers Security Charge
  Act](/eng/acts/A-10.5), the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1), the
  [Underused Housing Tax Act](/eng/acts/U-0.5) and the [Select Luxury
  Items Tax Act](/eng/acts/S-8.35) have been filed with the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of rebate

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of an application made by a person for a rebate under this
  Part, the Minister must, without delay, consider the application and
  assess the amount of the rebate, if any, payable to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reassessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may reassess or make an additional
  assessment of the amount of a rebate despite any previous assessment
  of the amount of the rebate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of overpayment of rebate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may assess, reassess or make an
  additional assessment of an amount payable by a person under section
  72 despite any previous assessment of the amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, on assessment under this section, the Minister
  determines that a rebate is payable to a person, the Minister must pay
  the rebate to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An amount under this section must not be rebated to a
  person at any time unless all returns of which the Minister has
  knowledge and that are required to be filed at or before that time by
  the person under this Part, the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15), the
  [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), the [Air Travellers Security Charge
  Act](/eng/acts/A-10.5), the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1), the
  [Underused Housing Tax Act](/eng/acts/U-0.5) and the [Select Luxury
  Items Tax Act](/eng/acts/S-8.35) have been filed with the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a rebate under this section is paid to a person,
  the Minister must pay interest at the prescribed rate to the person on
  the rebate for the period beginning on the day that is 30 days after
  the day on which the application for the rebate is filed with the
  Minister and ending on the day on which the rebate is paid.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of assessment

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After making an assessment under
  this Part, the Minister must send to the person assessed a notice of
  the assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of remainder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has assessed a person for an amount,
  any portion of that amount then remaining unpaid is payable to the
  Receiver General as of the date of the notice of assessment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period for assessments

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (7)
  and (10), no assessment in respect of any charge or other amount
  payable by a person under this Part must be made more than four years
  after it became payable by the person under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for assessment of rebate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (7) and (10), an
  assessment under subsection 109(1) of the amount of a rebate may be
  made at any time, but a reassessment or additional assessment under
  section 109 or an assessment under subsection 109(3) in respect of an
  amount paid or applied as a rebate or of an amount paid or applied as
  interest in respect of an amount paid or applied as a rebate is not to
  be made more than four years after the day the application for the
  rebate was filed in accordance with this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- objection or appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A variation of an assessment, or a reassessment, in
  respect of any charge or other amount payable under this Part by a
  person may be made at any time if the variation or reassessment is
  made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to give effect to a decision on an objection or
    appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the written consent of an appellant to dispose
    of an appeal; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to give effect to an alternative basis or argument
    advanced by the Minister under subsection (7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- neglect or fraud

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An assessment in respect of any matter may be made at
  any time if the person to be assessed has, in respect of that matter,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} made a misrepresentation that is attributable to
    their neglect, carelessness or wilful default; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} committed fraud with respect to a return or an
    application for a rebate filed under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- other period

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, in making an assessment, the Minister determines
  that a person has paid in respect of any matter an amount as or on
  account of a charge, or net charge, payable for a particular reporting
  period of the person that was in fact payable for another reporting
  period of the person, the Minister may at any time make an assessment
  for that other period in respect of that matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- adjustment to rebate

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the result of a reassessment on an objection to,
  or a decision on an appeal from, an assessment is to reduce the amount
  of a charge, or net charge, payable by a person and, by reason of the
  reduction, any rebate claimed by the person for a reporting period, or
  in an application for a rebate, should be reduced, the Minister may at
  any time assess or reassess that reporting period or that application
  for rebate, as the case may be, only for the purpose of taking the
  reduction of charge into account in respect of the rebate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative basis or argument

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may advance an alternative basis or
  argument in support of an assessment of a person, or in support of all
  or any portion of the total amount determined on assessment to be
  payable by a person under this Part, at any time after the period
  otherwise limited by subsection (1) or (2) for making the assessment
  unless, on an appeal under this Part,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is relevant evidence that the person is no
    longer able to adduce without leave of the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is not appropriate in the circumstances for the
    court to order that the evidence be adduced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a reassessment of a person is made that gives
  effect to an alternative basis or argument advanced by the Minister
  under subsection (7) in support of a particular assessment of the
  person, the Minister is not to reassess for an amount that is greater
  than the total amount of the particular assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (8) does not apply to any portion of an
  amount determined on reassessment that the Minister would be entitled
  to reassess under this Part at any time after the period otherwise
  limited by subsection (1) or (2) for making the reassessment if this
  Part were read without reference to subsection (7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- waiver

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An assessment in respect of any matter specified in
  a waiver filed under subsection (11) may be made at any time within
  the period specified in the waiver unless the waiver has been revoked
  under subsection (12), in which case an assessment may be made at any
  time during the 180 days that the waiver remains in effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing waiver

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Any person may, within the time otherwise limited by
  subsection (1) or (2) for an assessment, waive the application of
  subsection (1) or (2) by filing with the Minister a waiver in the
  prescribed form specifying the period for which, and the matter in
  respect of which, the person waives the application of that
  subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revoking waiver

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Any person that has filed a waiver may revoke it by
  filing with the Minister a notice of revocation of the waiver in the
  prescribed form and manner. The waiver remains in effect for 180 days
  after the day on which the notice is filed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of rebates and other amounts

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 165,
a rebate or other amount authorized to be paid under this Part may be
paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund at the time and in the manner
that the Minister considers appropriate.

#### [SUBDIVISION G]{.HLabel3}[Objections to Assessment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245578 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection to assessment

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person that has been assessed and that objects to the assessment may,
  within 90 days after the date of the notice of the assessment, file
  with the Minister a notice of objection in the prescribed form and
  manner setting out the reasons for the objection and all relevant
  facts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issue to be decided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of objection must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reasonably describe each issue to be decided;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify in respect of each issue the relief sought,
    expressed as the change in any amount that is relevant for the
    purposes of the assessment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide the facts and reasons relied on by the
    person in respect of each issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Late compliance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), if a notice of objection does
  not include the information required under paragraph (2)(b) or (c) in
  respect of an issue to be decided that is described in the notice, the
  Minister may, in writing, request the person to provide the
  information, and that paragraph is deemed to be complied with in
  respect of the issue if, within 60 days after the day on which the
  request is made, the person submits the information in writing to the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on objections

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if a person has filed a
  notice of objection to an assessment (in this subsection referred to
  as the "earlier assessment") and the Minister makes a particular
  assessment under subsection (8) as a result of the notice of
  objection, unless the earlier assessment was made in accordance with
  an order of a court vacating, varying or restoring an assessment or
  referring an assessment back to the Minister for reconsideration and
  reassessment, the person may object to the particular assessment in
  respect of an issue

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} only if the person complied with subsection (2) in
    the notice with respect to that issue; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} only with respect to the relief sought in respect
    of that issue as specified by the person in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- subsection (4)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not limit the right of the person
  to object to the particular assessment in respect of an issue that was
  part of the particular assessment and not part of the earlier
  assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on objections

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), no objection may be made by a
  person in respect of an issue for which the right of objection has
  been waived in writing by the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of objection

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may accept a notice of objection even
  though it was not filed in the prescribed form and manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of objection

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice of objection, the Minister
  must, without delay, reconsider the assessment and vacate or confirm
  it or make a reassessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiving reconsideration

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If, in a notice of objection, a person that wishes to
  appeal directly to the Tax Court of Canada requests the Minister not
  to reconsider the assessment objected to, the Minister may confirm the
  assessment without reconsideration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} After reconsidering an assessment under
  subsection (8) or confirming an assessment under subsection (9), the
  Minister must notify the person objecting to the assessment of the
  Minister's decision in writing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Minister

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  no objection to an assessment is filed under section 113 within the
  time limited under this Part, a person may make an application to the
  Minister to extend the time for filing a notice of objection and the
  Minister may grant the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out the reasons why the
  notice of objection was not filed within the time limited under this
  Part for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by delivering or mailing,
  to the Assistant Commissioner of the Appeals Branch of the Canada
  Revenue Agency, the application accompanied by a copy of the notice of
  objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defect in application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may accept an application even though it
  was not made in accordance with subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of Minister

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of an application, the Minister must,
  without delay, consider the application and grant or refuse it, and
  must notify the person of the decision in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of objection if application
  granted

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If an application is granted, the notice of objection
  is deemed to have been filed on the day of the decision of the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- grant of application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An application must not be granted under this section
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made within one year after the
    expiry of the time limited under this Part for objecting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under this Part for
      objecting, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to object to the assessment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application and
      the circumstances of the case, it would be just and equitable to
      grant the application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted it to be made.

#### [SUBDIVISION H]{.HLabel3}[Appeal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245627 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Tax Court of Canada

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person that has made an application under section 114 may apply to the
  Tax Court of Canada to have the application granted after either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has refused the application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 90 days have elapsed after the day on which the
    application was made and the Minister has not notified the person of
    the Minister's decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may not be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made after the expiry of 30
  days after the day on which the decision referred to in subsection
  114(5) was sent to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by filing in the Registry
  of the Tax Court of Canada, in accordance with the [Tax Court of
  Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2), three copies of the documents delivered or
  mailed under subsection 114(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to the Commissioner

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court of Canada must send a copy of the
  application to the Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Tax Court of Canada

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court of Canada may dispose of an application
  by dismissing or granting it and, in granting it, the Court may impose
  any terms that it considers just or order that the notice of objection
  be deemed to be a valid objection as of the date of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application to be granted

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An application must not be granted under this section
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application under subsection 114(1) was made
    within one year after the expiry of the time limited under this Part
    for objecting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under this Part for
      objecting, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to object to the assessment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application
      under this section and the circumstances of the case, it would be
      just and equitable to grant the application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application under subsection 114(1) was
      made as soon as circumstances permitted it to be made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Tax Court of Canada

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  that has filed a notice of objection to an assessment may appeal to
  the Tax Court of Canada to have the assessment vacated or a
  reassessment made after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has confirmed the assessment or has
    reassessed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 180 days have elapsed after the day on which the
    notice of objection was filed and the Minister has not notified the
    person that the Minister has vacated or confirmed the assessment or
    has reassessed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No appeal under subsection (1) may be instituted
  after the expiry of 90 days after the day on which notice that the
  Minister has reassessed or confirmed the assessment is sent to the
  person under subsection 113(10).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court of Canada may, on any terms that it
  sees fit, authorize a person that has instituted an appeal in respect
  of a matter to amend the appeal to include any further assessment in
  respect of the matter that the person is entitled under this section
  to appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time to appeal

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  no appeal to the Tax Court of Canada under section 116 has been
  instituted within the time limited by that section for doing so, a
  person may make an application to the Tax Court of Canada for an order
  extending the time within which an appeal may be instituted, and the
  Court may make an order extending the time for appealing and may
  impose any terms that it considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out the reasons why the
  appeal was not instituted within the time limited under section 116
  for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by filing in the Registry
  of the Tax Court of Canada, in accordance with the [Tax Court of
  Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2), three copies of the application together
  with three copies of the notice of appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Deputy Attorney General of
  Canada

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court of Canada must send a copy of the
  application to the office of the Deputy Attorney General of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When order to be made

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order must not be made under this section unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made within one year after the
    expiry of the time limited under section 116 for appealing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under section 116 for
      appealing, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to appeal,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application and
      the circumstances of the case, it would be just and equitable to
      grant the application,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted it to be made, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds for the appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on appeals to the Tax Court of
Canada

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 116, if a person has
  filed a notice of objection to an assessment, the person may appeal to
  the Tax Court of Canada to have the assessment vacated, or a
  reassessment made, only with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an issue in respect of which the person has
    complied with subsection 113(2) in the notice and the relief sought
    in respect of the issue as specified by the person in the notice; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an issue described in subsection 113(5) if the
    person was not required to file a notice of objection to the
    assessment that gave rise to the issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal if waiver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 116, a person may not appeal to the
  Tax Court of Canada to have an assessment vacated or varied in respect
  of an issue for which the right of objection or appeal has been waived
  in writing by the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Institution of appeals

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** An appeal to the Tax
Court of Canada under this Part must be instituted in accordance with
the [Tax Court of Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** The Tax Court of Canada
may dispose of an appeal from an assessment by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} dismissing it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} allowing it and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} vacating the assessment, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} referring the assessment back to the Minister for
    reconsideration and reassessment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to Tax Court of Canada

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister and another person agree in writing that a question
  arising under this Part, in respect of any assessment or proposed
  assessment of the person, should be determined by the Tax Court of
  Canada, that question must be determined by that Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time during consideration not to count

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of making an assessment of a person
  that agreed in writing to the determination of a question, filing a
  notice of objection to an assessment or instituting an appeal from an
  assessment, the time between the day on which proceedings are
  instituted in the Tax Court of Canada to have a question determined
  and the day on which the question is finally determined must not be
  counted in the computation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the four-year period referred to in subsection
    111(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which a notice of objection to an
    assessment may be filed under section 113; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period within which an appeal may be instituted
    under section 116.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference of common questions to Tax
Court of Canada

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is of the opinion that a question arising out of one and
  the same transaction or occurrence or series of transactions or
  occurrences is common to assessments or proposed assessments in
  respect of two or more persons, the Minister may apply to the Tax
  Court of Canada for a determination of the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the question in respect of which the Minister
    requests a determination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons that the Minister seeks to
    have bound by the determination; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the facts and reasons on which the Minister relies
    and on which the Minister based or intends to base assessments of
    each person named in the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the application must be served by the
  Minister on each of the persons named in it and on any other person
  that, in the opinion of the Tax Court of Canada, is likely to be
  affected by the determination of the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination by Tax Court of Canada of
  question

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Tax Court of Canada is satisfied that a
  determination of a question set out in an application will affect
  assessments or proposed assessments in respect of two or more persons
  that have been served with a copy of the application and that are
  named in an order of the Tax Court of Canada under this subsection, it
  may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if none of the persons named in the order has
    appealed from such an assessment, proceed to determine the question
    in any manner that it considers appropriate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more of the persons named in the order
    has or have appealed, make any order that it considers appropriate
    joining a party or parties to that appeal or those appeals and
    proceed to determine the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination final and conclusive

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), if a question set out in
  an application is determined by the Tax Court of Canada, the
  determination is final and conclusive for the purposes of any
  assessments of persons named by the Court under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a question set out in an application is determined
  by the Tax Court of Canada, the Minister or any of the persons that
  have been served with a copy of the application and that are named in
  an order of the Court under subsection (4) may, in accordance with the
  provisions of this Part, the [Tax Court of Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2)
  or the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7), as they relate to appeals
  from or applications for judicial review of decisions of the Tax Court
  of Canada, appeal from the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties to appeal

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The parties that are bound by a determination are
  parties to any appeal from the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time during consideration not counted

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of making an assessment of a person,
  filing a notice of objection to an assessment or instituting an appeal
  from an assessment, the periods described in subsection (9) must not
  be counted in the computation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the four-year period referred to in subsection
    111(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which a notice of objection to an
    assessment may be filed under section 113; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period within which an appeal may be instituted
    under section 116.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded periods

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The period that is not to be counted in the
  computation of the periods described in paragraphs (8)(a) to (c) is
  the time between the day on which an application that is made under
  this section is served on a person under subsection (3) and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person named in an order of the
    Tax Court of Canada under subsection (4), the day on which the
    determination becomes final and conclusive and not subject to any
    appeal; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other person, the day on which
    the person is served with a notice that the person has not been
    named in an order of the Tax Court of Canada under subsection (4).

#### [SUBDIVISION I]{.HLabel3}[Penalties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245730 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to file a return when required

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to file a return for a reporting period as and when required under this
Part is liable to pay a penalty equal to the sum of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to 1% of the total of all amounts
  each of which is an amount that is required to be paid for the
  reporting period and was not paid on the day on which the return was
  required to be filed, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount obtained when one quarter of the amount
  determined under paragraph (a) is multiplied by the number of complete
  months, not exceeding 12, from the day on which the return was
  required to be filed to the day on which the return is filed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- electronic payments

**[[123.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.1 .sectionLabel}** Every person that
fails to comply with subsection 86(2) is liable to a penalty equal to
\$100 for each such failure.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 94](#1409156-1420655){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1409156-1420655.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to file by electronic
transmission

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** In addition to any
other penalty under this Part, every person that fails to file a return
under this Part for a reporting period as required by subsection 89(3)
is liable to pay a penalty equal to an amount determined in prescribed
manner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiving or cancelling penalties

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on or before the day that is 10 calendar years after the
  end of a reporting period of a person, or on application by the person
  on or before that day, waive or cancel all or any portion of any
  penalty under this Part payable by the person in respect of the
  reporting period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest if amount waived or cancelled

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person has paid an amount of penalty and the
  Minister waives or cancels that amount under subsection (1), the
  Minister must pay interest on the amount paid by the person beginning
  on the day that is 30 days after the day on which the Minister
  received a request in a manner satisfactory to the Minister to apply
  that subsection and ending on the day on which the amount is rebated
  to the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to register

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** Every person that is
required to be registered under Division 4 of this Part but does not
apply for registration under that Division as and when required is
liable to pay a penalty of \$2,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General penalty

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to comply with any provision of this Part for which no other penalty is
specified is liable to pay a penalty of \$250.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to answer demand

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to file a return as and when required under a demand issued under
section 92 is liable to a penalty of \$500.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to provide information

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to provide any information or record as and when required under this
Part is liable to a penalty of \$250 for every failure unless, in the
case of information required in respect of another person, a reasonable
effort was made by the person to obtain the information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to provide information

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to report an amount prescribed by regulation, or to provide information
prescribed by regulation, in a return prescribed by regulation as and
when required, or that misstates such an amount or such information in
such a return, is liable to pay a penalty, in addition to any other
penalty under this Part, equal to an amount determined in prescribed
manner for each such failure or misstatement by the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False statements or omissions

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** Every person that
knowingly, or under circumstances amounting to gross negligence, makes
or participates in, assents to or acquiesces in the making of a false
statement or omission in a return, application, form, certificate,
statement, invoice or answer (each of which is in this section referred
to as a "return") is liable to pay a penalty of the greater of \$500 and
25% of the total of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the false statement or omission is relevant to the
  determination of an amount payable under this Part by the person, the
  amount, if any, by which

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the amount payable

  exceeds

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the amount that would be payable by the person if
    the amount payable were determined on the basis of the information
    provided in the return, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the false statement or omission is relevant to the
  determination of a rebate or any other payment that may be obtained
  under this Part, the amount, if any, by which

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the amount that would be the rebate or other
    payment payable to the person if the rebate or other payment were
    determined on the basis of the information provided in the return

  exceeds

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the rebate or other payment payable
    to the person.

#### [SUBDIVISION J]{.HLabel3}[Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence for failure to file return or to
comply with demand or order

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person that fails to file or
  make a return as and when required under this Part or that fails to
  comply with an obligation under subsection 104(6) or (9) or section
  106, or an order made under section 137, is guilty of an offence and,
  in addition to any penalty otherwise provided, is liable on summary
  conviction to a fine of not less than \$2,000 and not more than
  \$40,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding 12 months, or to
  both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person that is convicted of an offence under
  subsection (1) for a failure to comply with a provision of this Part
  is not liable to pay a penalty under this Part for the same failure,
  unless a notice of assessment for the penalty was issued before the
  information or complaint giving rise to the conviction was laid or
  made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences for false or deceptive statement

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes, or participates in, assents to or acquiesces
    in the making of, a false or deceptive statement in a return,
    application, certificate, statement, document, record or answer
    filed or made as required under this Part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of evading payment of any amount
    payable under this Part, or obtaining a rebate or other payment
    payable under this Part to which the person is not entitled,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} destroys, alters, mutilates, conceals or
      otherwise disposes of any records of a person, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} makes, or assents to or acquiesces in the making
      of, a false or deceptive entry, or omits, or assents to or
      acquiesces in the omission, to enter a material particular in the
      records of a person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} intentionally, in any manner, evades or attempts to
    evade compliance with this Part or payment of an amount payable
    under this Part;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} intentionally, in any manner, obtains or attempts
    to obtain a rebate or other payment payable under this Part to which
    the person is not entitled; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} conspires with any person to commit an offence
    described in any of paragraphs (a) to (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person that commits an offence under
  subsection (1) is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
  conviction and, in addition to any penalty otherwise provided, is
  liable to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a fine of not less than 50%, and not more than
    200%, of the amount payable that was sought to be evaded, or of the
    rebate or other payment sought, or, if the amount that was sought to
    be evaded cannot be ascertained, a fine of not less than \$2,000 and
    not more than \$40,000;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for a term not exceeding two years; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} both a fine referred to in paragraph (a) and
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecution on indictment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person that is charged with an offence
  described in subsection (1) may, at the election of the Attorney
  General of Canada, be prosecuted on indictment and, if convicted, is,
  in addition to any penalty otherwise provided, liable to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a fine of not less than 100%, and not more than
    200%, of the amount payable that was sought to be evaded, or of the
    rebate or other payment sought, or, if the amount that was sought to
    be evaded cannot be ascertained, a fine of not less than \$5,000 and
    not more than \$100,000;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} both a fine referred to in paragraph (a) and
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty on conviction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person that is convicted of an offence under this
  section is not liable to pay a penalty imposed under this Part for the
  same evasion or attempt unless a notice of assessment for that penalty
  was issued before the information or complaint giving rise to the
  conviction was laid or made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of appeal

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, in any appeal under this Part, substantially the
  same facts are at issue as those that are at issue in a prosecution
  under this section, the Minister may file a stay of proceedings with
  the Tax Court of Canada and, upon that filing, the proceedings before
  the Tax Court of Canada are stayed pending a final determination of
  the outcome of the prosecution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence --- confidential information

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a
  fine not exceeding \$5,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
  12 months, or to both, if that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes subsection 107(2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} knowingly contravenes an order made under
    subsection 107(12).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence --- confidential information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person to whom confidential information has
  been provided for a particular purpose under subsection 107(6) or (7)
  and that for any other purpose knowingly uses, provides to any person,
  allows the provision to any person of, or allows any person access to,
  that information is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction to a fine not exceeding \$5,000 or to imprisonment for a
  term not exceeding 12 months, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[confidential
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[confidential
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
  subsection 107(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 12, s. 186 "134"
- [2024, c. 17, s. 148](#1467441-1480201){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467441-1480201.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-134-20180621.html "Link to previous version of section 134"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to pay charge

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** Every person that
intentionally fails to pay a charge as and when required under this Part
is guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction and liable, in
addition to any penalty or interest otherwise provided, to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a fine not exceeding the aggregate of \$1,000 and an
  amount equal to 20% of the amount of charge that should have been
  paid;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} both a fine referred to in paragraph (a) and
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General offence

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Every person that fails
to comply with any provision of this Part for which no other offence is
specified in this Part is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
conviction and liable to a fine of not more than \$100,000 or to
imprisonment for a term of not more than 12 months, or to both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance orders

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** If a person is
convicted by a court of an offence for a failure to comply with a
provision of this Part, the court may make any order that it deems
appropriate to enforce compliance with the provision.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers of corporations, etc.

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** If a person other than
an individual commits an offence under this Part, every officer,
director or representative of the person that directed, authorized,
assented to, acquiesced in or participated in the commission of the
offence is a party to and guilty of the offence and liable on conviction
to the punishment provided for the offence, whether or not the person
has been prosecuted or convicted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to decrease punishment

**[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** Despite the [Criminal
Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other law, the court has, in any
prosecution or proceeding under this Part, neither the power to impose
less than the minimum fine fixed under this Part nor the power to
suspend sentence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information or complaint

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  information or complaint under this Part may be laid or made by any
  officer of the Canada Revenue Agency, by a member of the Royal
  Canadian Mounted Police or by any person authorized to do so by the
  Minister and, if an information or complaint purports to have been
  laid or made under this Part, it is deemed to have been laid or made
  by a person so authorized by the Minister and must not be called in
  question for lack of authority of the informant or complainant, except
  by the Minister or a person acting for the Minister or for Her Majesty
  in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Two or more offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An information or complaint in respect of an offence
  under this Part may be for one or more offences, and no information,
  complaint, warrant, conviction or other proceeding in a prosecution
  under this Part is objectionable or insufficient by reason of the fact
  that it relates to two or more offences.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Territorial jurisdiction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An information or complaint in respect of an offence
  under this Part may be heard, tried or determined by any court having
  territorial jurisdiction where the accused is resident, carrying on a
  commercial activity, found, apprehended or in custody, despite that
  the matter of the information or complaint did not arise within that
  territorial jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation of prosecutions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No proceeding by way of summary conviction in respect
  of an offence under this Part may be instituted more than five years
  after the day on which the subject matter of the proceedings arose,
  unless the prosecutor and the defendant agree that they may be
  instituted after the five years.

#### [SUBDIVISION K]{.HLabel3}[Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-245823 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By whom

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person authorized by the Minister to do so may, at all reasonable
  times, for any purpose related to the administration or enforcement of
  this Part, inspect, audit or examine the records, processes, property
  or premises of a person that may be relevant in determining the
  obligations of that or any other person under this Part, or the amount
  of any rebate to which that or any other person is entitled under this
  Part and whether that person or any other person is in compliance with
  this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of authorized person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the authorized person may,
  at all reasonable times, for any purpose related to the administration
  or enforcement of this Part

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter any place in which the authorized person
    reasonably believes the person keeps or should keep records, carries
    on any activity to which this Part applies or does anything in
    relation to that activity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require any person to give the authorized person
    all reasonable assistance, to answer all proper questions relating
    to the administration or enforcement of this Part and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to attend with the authorized person at a place
      designated by the authorized person, or by video-conference or by
      another form of electronic communication, and to answer the
      questions orally, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to answer the questions in writing, in any form
      specified by the authorized person; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} require any person to give the authorized person
    all reasonable assistance with anything the authorized person is
    authorized to do under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prior authorization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If any place referred to in subsection (2) is a
  dwelling-house, the authorized person may not enter that
  dwelling-house without the consent of the occupant, except under the
  authority of a warrant issued under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant to enter dwelling-house

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A judge may issue a warrant authorizing a person to
  enter a dwelling-house subject to the conditions specified in the
  warrant if, on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application by the
  Minister, a judge is satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds to believe that the
    dwelling-house is a place referred to in subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry into the dwelling-house is necessary for any
    purpose related to the administration or enforcement of this Part;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry into the dwelling-house has been, or there
    are reasonable grounds to believe that entry will be, refused.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders if entry not authorized

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the judge is not satisfied that entry into the
  dwelling-house is necessary for any purpose related to the
  administration or enforcement of this Part, the judge may, to the
  extent that access was or may be expected to be refused and that a
  record or property is or may be expected to be kept in the
  dwelling-house,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the occupant of the dwelling-house to provide
    a person with reasonable access to any record or property that is or
    should be kept in the dwelling-house; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make any other order that is appropriate in the
    circumstances to carry out the purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[dwelling-house]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
  [[dwelling-house]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the whole or any part of
  a building or structure that is kept or occupied as a permanent or
  temporary residence, and includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a building within the curtilage of a dwelling-house
    that is connected to it by a doorway or by a covered and enclosed
    passageway; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a unit that is designed to be mobile and to be used
    as a permanent or temporary residence and that is being used as such
    a residence.
