    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} maintaining records on any relevant matter,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reporting periodically to the enforcement
      officer, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} submitting to the enforcement officer any
      information, proposal or plan specified by the enforcement officer
      and setting out any action to be taken by the person with respect
      to the subject matter of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order must be provided in the form of a written
  notice and must set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of each person to whom it is directed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provision of this Part or the regulations that
    is alleged to have been or that is likely to be contravened;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the relevant facts surrounding the alleged
    contravention;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whatever it is to be stopped or the measures to be
    taken;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the time or the day when each measure is to begin
    or the period during which it is to be carried out;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (3), the duration of the
    order;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a statement that a request for a review may be made
    to the Chief Review Officer; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the period within which a request for a review may
    be made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The maximum duration of an order is 180 days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order is not a statutory instrument for the
  purposes of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of intent

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  enforcement officer must, before issuing an order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide an oral or a written notice of the intent
    of the enforcement officer to issue the order to every person to
    whom the order will be directed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow a reasonable opportunity in the circumstances
    for the person to make oral representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice of intent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of intent to issue an order must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the purpose of the notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to the statutory authority under which
    the order will be issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provision of this Part or the regulations that
    is alleged to have been or that is likely to be contravened; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the person to whom the order will
    be directed may make oral representations to the enforcement officer
    within the period stated in the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with the order

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person to whom an order is directed must, on receipt of the order or a
  copy of it, comply with the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No bar to proceedings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The issuance of or compliance with an order in
  respect of a person's alleged contravention of this Part or the
  regulations is not a bar to any proceedings against the person under
  this Part or under any other Act in relation to the alleged
  contravention.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by enforcement officer

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  any person to whom an order is directed fails to take any measures
  specified in the order, an enforcement officer may take the measures
  or authorize a third party to take the measures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to property

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer or third party that is
  authorized to take measures under subsection (1) may enter and have
  access to any place or property, other than a dwelling-house, and may
  do any reasonable things that may be necessary in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a third party is authorized to take measures under
  subsection (1), no action or other proceeding of a civil nature may be
  brought against the third party in respect of any act or omission
  committed in good faith in taking those measures.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of reasonable costs and expenses
by Her Majesty

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Her
  Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs and expenses of and
  incidental to taking any measures under subsection 218(1) from any
  person to whom the order is directed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonably incurred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses referred to in subsection (1)
  must only be recovered to the extent that they can be established to
  have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the order is directed to more than one person,
  they are jointly and severally, or solidarily, liable for the costs
  and expenses referred to in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A claim under this section may be sued for and
  recovered by Her Majesty in right of Canada with costs in proceedings
  brought or taken in respect of the claim in the name of Her Majesty in
  right of Canada in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse or indemnity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit or restrict any right of
  recourse or indemnity that a person may have against any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If events giving rise to a claim under this section
  occur, no proceedings in respect of the claim may be instituted after
  five years from the date on which the events occur or came to the
  knowledge of the Minister, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's certificate

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to have been issued by the
  Minister certifying the day on which the events giving rise to a claim
  under this section came to the knowledge of the Minister must be
  received in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary, the document must be considered as proof of that fact
  without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the document.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation or cancellation of order

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  any time before a notice requesting a review of an order is received
  by the Chief Review Officer, the enforcement officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} amend or suspend a condition of the order, or add
    condition to, or delete a condition from, the order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cancel the order;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} correct a clerical error in the order; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} extend the duration of the order up to the maximum
    duration set out in subsection 215(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of intent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The enforcement officer must, before exercising a
  power under paragraph (1)(a) or (d),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide an oral or a written notice of the intent
    of the enforcement officer to do so to every person to whom the
    order is directed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow a reasonable opportunity in the circumstances
    for the person to make oral representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice of intent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A notice of intent to exercise a power under
  paragraph (1)(a) or (d) must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the purpose of the notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to the statutory authority under which
    the power will be exercised;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provision of this Part or the regulations that
    is alleged to have been or that is likely to be contravened; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the person to whom the order is
    directed may make oral representations to the enforcement officer
    within the period stated in the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may make
regulations respecting reporting under subparagraph 215(1)(c)(ii) and
respecting representations made to enforcement officers under subsection
216(1) or 220(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person to whom an order is directed may, by notice in writing given to
  the Chief Review Officer within 30 days after receipt by the person of
  the written order or a copy of it, make a request to the Chief Review
  Officer for a review of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period for request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Review Officer may extend the period within
  which a request for a review may be made if, in the Chief Review
  Officer's opinion, it is in the public interest to do so.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of order

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** Sections 257 to 264 and
266 to 271 of the [Canadian Environmental Protection Act,
1999](/eng/acts/C-15.31) apply, with any modifications that the
circumstances require, to a review of any order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** No action or other
proceeding of a civil nature may be brought against a review officer in
respect of anything that is done or omitted to be done in good faith in
the exercise of their powers or the performance of their duties and
functions under section 223.

#### [Voluntary Reports]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246760 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary reports

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person has knowledge of the commission or reasonable likelihood of the
  commission of an offence under this Part, but is not required to
  report the matter under this Part, the person may report any
  information relating to the offence or likely offence to an
  enforcement officer or any person to whom a report may be made under
  this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person making the report may request that their
  identity, and any information that could reasonably be expected to
  reveal their identity, not be disclosed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for confidentiality

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person must not disclose or cause to be disclosed
  the identity of a person who makes a request under subsection (2) or
  any information that could reasonably be expected to reveal their
  identity unless the person authorizes the disclosure in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee protection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament, an employer must
  not dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or otherwise
  disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of employment,
  with the intent to retaliate against the employee because the employee
  has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} made a report under subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} acting in good faith and on the basis of reasonable
    belief, refused or stated an intention of refusing to do anything
    that is an offence under this Part; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} acting in good faith and on the basis of reasonable
    belief, done or stated an intention of doing anything that is
    required to be done by or under this Part.

#### [Application for Investigation of Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246773 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for investigation by Minister

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who is resident in Canada and at least 18 years of age may
  apply to the Minister for an investigation of any offence under this
  Part that the individual alleges has occurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement to accompany application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a solemn affirmation or
  declaration

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stating the name and address of the applicant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} stating that the applicant is at least 18 years old
    and a resident of Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} stating the nature of the alleged offence and the
    name of each person alleged to be involved; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} containing a concise statement of the evidence
    supporting the allegations of the applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by regulation, prescribe the form
  in which an application under this section is required to be made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation by Minister

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
acknowledge receipt of the application within 20 days after the receipt
and must investigate all matters that he or she considers necessary to
determine the facts relating to the alleged offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progress reports

**[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** Starting on the day on
which the Minister acknowledges receipt of the application, he or she
must report to the applicant every 90 days on the progress of the
investigation and the action, if any, that the Minister has taken or
proposes to take, and the Minister must include in the report an
estimate of the time required to complete the investigation or to
implement the action. A report is not however required if the
investigation is discontinued before the end of the 90 days.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending evidence to Attorney General of
Canada

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** At any stage of an
investigation, the Minister may send any documents or other evidence to
the Attorney General of Canada for consideration of whether an offence
has been or is about to be committed under this Part and for any action
that the Attorney General may wish to take.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuation of investigation

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may discontinue an investigation if he or she is of the
  opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the alleged offence does not require further
    investigation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the investigation does not substantiate the alleged
    offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an investigation is discontinued, the Minister
  must prepare a report in writing that describes the information
  obtained during the investigation and states the reasons for its
  discontinuation and he or she must send a copy of the report to the
  applicant and to any person whose conduct was investigated. A copy of
  the report that is sent to a person whose conduct was investigated
  must not disclose the name or address of the applicant or any other
  personal information about them.

#### [Injunctions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246798 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injunctions

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  on the application of the Minister, it appears to a court of competent
  jurisdiction that a person has done or is about to do or is likely to
  do any act or thing constituting or directed toward the commission of
  an offence under this Part, the court may issue an injunction ordering
  any person named in the application

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to refrain from doing any act or thing that it
    appears to the court may constitute or be directed toward the
    commission of an offence under this Part; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to do any act or thing that it appears to the court
    may prevent the commission of an offence under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least 48 hours before the injunction is issued,
  notice of the application must be served on persons named in the
  application, unless the urgency of the situation is such that the
  delay involved in serving the notice would not be in the public
  interest.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-246806 .Subheading}

#### [Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246807 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes section 208 or subsection 217(1) or
    225(4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} knowingly contravenes section 209;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} contravenes any provision of a regulation that is
    designated by regulations made under section 246;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} contravenes an order of a court made under this
    Part;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} knowingly, with respect to any matter related to
    this Part, provides any person with any false or misleading
    information or samples;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} knowingly, with respect to any matter related to
    this Part, files a document that contains false or misleading
    information; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} knowingly, destroys, alters, mutilates, conceals or
    otherwise disposes of any records that are kept and retained under
    this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- individuals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every individual who commits an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$15,000 and not more than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a
      term of not more than three years, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$30,000 and not more than \$2,000,000 or to
      imprisonment for a term of not more than three years, or to both;
      or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$5,000 and not more than \$300,000 or to imprisonment for a term
      of not more than six months, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$10,000 and not more than \$600,000 or to
      imprisonment for a term of not more than six months, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person, other than an individual or an
  organization referred to in subsection (4), that commits an offence
  under subsection (1) is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$500,000 and not more than \$6,000,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$1,000,000 and not more than \$12,000,000; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$100,000 and not more than \$4,000,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$200,000 and not more than \$8,000,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- small revenue organizations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every organization that commits an offence under
  subsection (1) and that the court determines under section 234 to be a
  small revenue organization is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$75,000 and not more than \$4,000,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$150,000 and not more than \$8,000,000; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not less than
      \$25,000 and not more than \$2,000,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not less than \$50,000 and not more than \$4,000,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes any provision of this Part, other than
    a provision the contravention of which is an offence under paragraph
    232(1)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contravenes any provision of a regulation made
    under this Part, other than a provision the contravention of which
    is an offence under paragraph 232(1)(c);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to any matter related to this Part,
    provides any person with any false or misleading information or
    samples; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} with respect to any matter related to this Part,
    files a document that contains false or misleading information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- individuals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every individual who commits an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$100,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$200,000; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$25,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$50,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person, other than an individual or a
  organization referred to in subsection (4), that commits an offence
  under subsection (1) is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$500,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$1,000,000; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$250,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$500,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- small revenue organizations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every organization that commits an offence under
  subsection (1) and that the court determines under section 234 to be a
  small revenue organization is liable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$250,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$500,000; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$50,000, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second or subsequent offence, to a fine of
      not more than \$100,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- provision of compensation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a person is found guilty of contravening
  subsection 174(1) or paragraph 178(1)(a), the court must, in addition
  to any penalty that may be imposed under this section, order the
  offender to provide compensation, at the rates set out in subsection
  174(4), for the excess emissions for which no compensation was
  provided and for which no compliance units were withdrawn.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of small revenue
organization status

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 232 and 233, a court may determine an organization to be a
small revenue organization if the court is satisfied that the
organization's gross revenues for the 12 months immediately before the
day on which the subject matter of the proceedings arose --- or, if it
arose on more than one day, for the 12 months immediately before the
first day on which the subject matter of the proceedings arose --- were
not more than \$5,000,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief from minimum fine

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** The court may impose a
fine that is less than the minimum amount provided for in subsection
232(2), (3) or (4) if it is satisfied, on the basis of evidence
submitted to the court, that the minimum fine would cause undue
financial hardship. The court must provide reasons if it imposes a fine
that is less than the minimum amount provided for in any of those
subsections.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming --- second and subsequent offence

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsections 232(2) to (4) and 233(2) to (4), a
  conviction for a particular offence under this Part is deemed to be a
  conviction for a second or subsequent offence if the court is
  satisfied that the offender has been previously convicted --- under
  any Act of Parliament, or any Act of the legislature of a province,
  that relates to the control or pricing of greenhouse gas emissions ---
  of a substantially similar offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only to previous convictions
  on indictment and to previous convictions on summary conviction, and
  to previous convictions under any similar procedure under any Act of
  the legislature of a province.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional fine

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** If a person is
convicted of an offence and the court is satisfied that, as a result of
the commission of the offence, the person acquired any property, benefit
or advantage, the court must order the offender to pay an additional
fine in an amount equal to the court's estimation of the value of that
property, benefit or advantage. The additional fine may exceed the
maximum amount of any fine that may otherwise be imposed under this
Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to shareholders

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** If a corporation that
has shareholders is convicted of an offence under this Part, the court
must make an order directing the corporation to notify its shareholders,
in the manner and within the time directed by the court, of the facts
relating to the commission of the offence and of the details of the
punishment imposed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** No proceedings by way
of summary conviction in respect of an offence under this Part may be
instituted more than five years after the day on which the subject
matter of the proceedings arose, unless the prosecutor and the defendant
agree that they may be instituted after the five years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence for each tonne

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** If the offence of
contravening subsection 174(1) or paragraph 178(1)(a) is committed, the
person that committed the offence is liable to be convicted for a
separate offence for each CO~2~e tonne of a greenhouse gas that is
emitted over the applicable emissions limit for which no compensation is
provided by the increased-rate compensation deadline.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, by regulation, prescribe the manner in which the proceeds or any
part of the proceeds resulting from the payment of a fine or the
execution of an order in relation to an offence under this Part must be
distributed in order to reimburse any person, government or body that
has commenced the proceedings in respect of the offence for costs
incurred by that person, government or body in respect of the
prosecution of the offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of senior officers

- **[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an organization commits an offence under this Part, a senior officer
  of the organization who directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced
  in or participated in the commission of the offence is a party to and
  guilty of the offence, and is liable on conviction to the penalty
  provided for by this Part for an individual in respect of the offence
  committed by the organization, whether or not the organization has
  been prosecuted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[senior
  officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[senior
  officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a director, partner, employee,
  member, agent, mandatary or contractor who plays an important role in
  the establishment of an organization's policies or is responsible for
  managing an important aspect of its activities and, in the case of a
  corporation, includes its chief executive officer and its chief
  financial officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of offence

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** In any prosecution of
an offence under this Part, other than an offence of contravening
section 208 or subsection 225(4) or an offence under any of paragraphs
232(1)(b) and (e) to (g), it is sufficient proof of the offence to
establish that it was committed by a director, partner, employee,
member, agent, mandatary or contractor of the accused organization,
whether or not they are identified or prosecuted for the offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** A person must not be
found guilty of an offence under this Part, other than an offence of
contravening section 208 or subsection 225(4) or an offence under any of
paragraphs 232(1)(b) and (e) to (g), if the person establishes that they
exercised all due diligence to prevent its commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of analyst

- **[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a certificate of an analyst that states the result of an analysis or
  examination and includes any related statement is admissible in
  evidence in any prosecution for an offence under this Part and, in the
  absence of evidence to the contrary, is proof of the statements
  contained in the certificate without proof of the signature or the
  official character of the person appearing to have signed the
  certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance of analyst

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The party against whom a certificate of an analyst is
  produced may, with leave of the court, require the attendance of the
  analyst for the purposes of cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of an analyst must not be received in
  evidence unless the party intending to produce it has given to the
  party against whom it is intended to be produced reasonable notice of
  that intention together with a copy of the certificate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, by regulation, designate provisions of regulations made under this
Part for the purposes of paragraph 232(1)(c).

#### [Sentencing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246912 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fundamental purpose

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** The fundamental purpose
of sentencing for offences under this Part is to contribute --- in light
of the risks posed by climate change to the environment, including its
biological diversity, to human health and safety and to economic
prosperity --- to respect for laws related to the pricing of greenhouse
gas emissions through the imposition of just sanctions that have as
their objectives

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the deterrence of the offender and any other person
  from committing offences under this Part;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the denunciation of the unlawful conduct; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the reinforcement of the "polluter pays" principle.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Principles

- **[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the principles and factors that the court is otherwise
  required to consider, including those set out in sections 718.1 to
  718.21 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the court must consider
  the following principles when sentencing a person that is convicted of
  an offence under this Part:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the fine should be increased to
    account for every aggravating factor associated with the offence,
    including the aggravating factors set out in subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the fine should reflect the gravity
    of each aggravating factor associated with the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The aggravating factors are the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender committed the offence intentionally or
    recklessly;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offender failed to take reasonable steps to
    prevent the commission of the offence despite having the financial
    means to do so;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by committing the offence or failing to take action
    to prevent its commission, the offender increased revenue or
    decreased costs or intended to increase revenue or decrease costs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the offender committed the offence despite having
    been warned by an enforcement officer of the circumstances that
    subsequently became the subject of the offence;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the offender has a history of non-compliance with
    federal or provincial legislation that relates to the control or
    pricing of greenhouse gas emissions; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} after the commission of the offence, the offender

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} attempted to conceal its commission,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} failed to take prompt action to prevent,
      mitigate or remediate its effects, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} failed to take prompt action to reduce the risk
      of committing similar offences in the future.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of aggravating factor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The absence of an aggravating factor set out in
  subsection (2) is not a mitigating factor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the court is satisfied of the existence of one or
  more of the aggravating factors set out in subsection (2) but decides
  not to increase the amount of the fine because of that factor, the
  court must give reasons for that decision.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders of court

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an offender has been convicted of an offence under this Part, in
  addition to any other punishment that may be imposed under this Part,
  the court may, having regard to the nature of the offence and the
  circumstances surrounding its commission, make an order having any or
  all of the following effects:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the offender from doing any act or
    engaging in any activity that may result in the continuation or
    repetition of the offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to implement a greenhouse
    gas emissions control or reduction system that meets a recognized
    Canadian or international standard;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to have an audit conducted
    by a person of a class and at the times specified by the court and
    directing the offender to remedy any deficiencies revealed during
    the audit;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to pay to Her Majesty in
    right of Canada an amount of money that the court considers
    appropriate for the purpose of promoting the control or reduction of
    greenhouse gas emissions or mitigating the effects of climate change
    caused by those emissions;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to publish, in the manner
    specified by the court, the facts relating to the commission of the
    offence and the details of the punishment imposed, including any
    orders made under this subsection;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to notify, at the offender's
    own cost and in the manner specified by the court, any person
    aggrieved or affected by the offender's conduct of the facts
    relating to the commission of the offence and of the details of the
    punishment imposed, including any orders made under this subsection;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to post any bond or pay any
    amount of money into court that will ensure compliance with any
    order made under this section;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to submit to the Minister,
    on application by the Minister made within three years after the
    date of conviction, any information with respect to the offender's
    activities that the court considers appropriate and just in the
    circumstances;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to compensate any person,
    monetarily or otherwise, in whole or in part, for the cost of any
    remedial or preventive action taken, caused to be taken or to be
    taken as a result of the act or omission that constituted the
    offence, including costs of assessing appropriate remedial or
    preventive action;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to perform community
    service, subject to any reasonable conditions that may be imposed in
    the order;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to pay, in the manner
    prescribed by the court, an amount for the purpose of conducting
    research into climate change;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to pay, in the manner
    prescribed by the court, an amount to groups concerned with climate
    change --- including groups concerned with the effects of climate
    change on the Indigenous peoples of Canada and on northern, coastal
    or remote communities --- to assist in their work;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to pay, in the manner
    prescribed by the court, an amount to an educational institution
    including for scholarships for students enrolled in studies related
    to climate change;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} requiring the offender to comply with any other
    conditions that the court considers appropriate in the circumstances
    for securing the offender's good conduct and for deterring the
    offender and any other person from committing offences under this
    Part;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} requiring the offender to remit compliance units to
    the Minister or any other person; and

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the offender from entering into
    transactions involving compliance units during any period that the
    court considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an offender fails to comply with an order made
  under paragraph (1)(e), the Minister may, in the manner that the court
  directed the offender to do so, publish the facts relating to the
  commission of the offence and the details of the punishment imposed
  and recover the costs of publication from the offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to Her Majesty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the court makes an order under paragraph (1)(d)
  or (i) directing an offender to pay an amount to Her Majesty in right
  of Canada, or if the Minister incurs publication costs under
  subsection (2), the amount or the costs, as the case may be,
  constitute a debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and may be
  recovered in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the court makes an order under subsection (1)
  directing an offender to pay an amount to a person other than to Her
  Majesty in right of Canada and the amount is not paid without delay,
  the person may, by filing the order, enter as a judgment, in the
  superior court of the province in which the trial was held, the amount
  ordered to be paid, and that judgment is enforceable against the
  offender in the same manner as if it were a judgment rendered against
  the offender in that court in civil proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retirement of compliance units

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the court makes an order under paragraph (1)(o),
  any compliance unit that is remitted to the Minister in accordance
  with the order is retired from circulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coming into force and duration

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) comes into force
  on the day on which it is made or on any other day that the court may
  determine and must not continue in force for more than three years
  after that day unless the court provides otherwise in the order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspended sentence

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person is convicted of an offence and the court suspends the passing
  of sentence under paragraph 731(1)(a) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the court may, in addition to any probation
  order made under that Act, make an order containing one or more of the
  prohibitions, directions or requirements referred to in subsection
  249(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imposition of sentence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person does not comply with the order or is
  convicted of another offence within three years after the order is
  made, the court may, on the application of the prosecution, impose any
  sentence that could have been imposed if the passing of sentence had
  not been suspended.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of fines

- **[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations made under
  section 241, all fines received by the Receiver General in respect of
  the commission of an offence under this Part or any amount received in
  accordance with an order under paragraph 249(1)(d) are to be credited
  to the Environmental Damages Fund, an account in the accounts of
  Canada, and used for purposes related to protecting, conserving or
  restoring the environment or for administering that Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, purposes related to
  protecting, conserving or restoring the environment include promoting
  the control or reduction of greenhouse gas emissions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations of court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A court imposing a fine may recommend to the Minister
  that all or a portion of the fine credited to the Environmental
  Damages Fund be paid to a person or an organization, including any
  entity that represents the interests of one or more of the Indigenous
  peoples of Canada, specified by the court for a purpose referred to in
  subsection (1).

#### [Registry]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246979 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of information about
contraventions

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of encouraging compliance with this Part, the Minister
  must keep, in a registry accessible to the public, information about
  all convictions of organizations for offences under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Information in the registry is to be kept for a
  minimum of five years.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Miscellaneous]{.HTitleText2} {#h-246985 .Subheading}

#### [Agreements Respecting Administration and Enforcement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246986 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negotiation of agreement

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may negotiate an agreement respecting the administration and
  enforcement of this Part with any person, any government in Canada,
  the government of a foreign state or a political subdivision of a
  foreign state, any international organization or any institution of a
  government or an international organization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of final agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish an agreement made under
  subsection (1), or give notice of its availability, in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action not limited by agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made under this section must not limit
  or restrict the carrying out of any action the Minister deems
  necessary for the administration and enforcement of this Part,
  including the conduct of inspections or investigations.

#### [Confidentiality]{.HTitleText3} {#h-246994 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** A person that provides
information to the Minister under this Part may submit with the
information a written request, with supporting justifications, that the
information be treated as confidential for one or more of the following
reasons:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information constitutes a trade secret;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of the information would likely cause
  material financial loss to, or prejudice to the competitive position
  of, the person; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of the information would likely
  interfere with contractual or other negotiations being conducted by
  the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional justification

- **[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, after studying the reasons provided under section 254,
  require the person in question to provide in writing, within a period
  specified by the Minister, additional justification for the request
  for confidentiality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to accept or reject the
  request, the Minister must consider whether the reasons are
  well-founded and, if they are, the Minister may nevertheless reject
  the request if the Minister considers that the public interest in the
  disclosure of the information outweighs in importance any material
  financial loss or prejudice to the competitive position of the person
  that provided the information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of request

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister accepts the request, he or she must
  not disclose the information unless the disclosure

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is made with the written consent of the person that
    made the request;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is made to the Canada Revenue Agency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is made to the Minister of Finance for the purpose
    of developing policy related to the pricing of greenhouse gas
    emissions;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is necessary for the purposes of the administration
    or enforcement of this Part; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} is made under an agreement or arrangement between
    the Government of Canada or any of its institutions and any other
    government in Canada, the government of a foreign state or a
    political subdivision of a foreign state, or an international
    organization or any of its institutions, or between the Minister and
    any other minister of the Crown in right of Canada, in which the
    government, international organization, institution or other
    minister undertakes to keep the information confidential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection of request

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister rejects the request,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has the right to ask the Federal Court
    to review the matter within 30 days after the day on which the
    person is notified that the request has been rejected or within any
    further time that the Court may, before the expiry of those 30 days,
    fix; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister must advise the person of the
    rejection, of the Minister's intention to disclose the information
    and of the person's right to ask the Federal Court to review the
    matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable provisions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the person asks the Federal Court to review a
  matter under paragraph (4)(a), sections 45 to 47 of the [Access to
  Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1) apply, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require, in respect of a request for a review under that
  paragraph as if it were an application made under section 44 of that
  Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations specifying the information to be provided in a
request for confidentiality.

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-247020 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
regulations made under this Part may distinguish among any province or
area and among any class of persons, equipment, facilities, activities
or greenhouse gas emission sources, including fuels.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference --- limitation
removed

**[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** The limitation set out
in paragraph 18.‍1(2)‍(a) of the [Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22), to the effect that a document must be incorporated
as it exists on a particular date, does not apply to any power to make
regulations under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations not mandatory

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** The following
provisions apply in the absence of any regulations referred to in those
provisions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsections 172(1) and (3);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 175 and 179;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 180(2);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} section 182;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} subsection 187(3); and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} subsections 188(1), (2) and (4).

#### [[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)]{.HTitleText3} {#h-247033 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

**[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
an excess emissions charge is not a [[fee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as
defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4).

#### [Review]{.HTitleText3} {#h-247036 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must undertake a review of sections 232 to 252 each time the
  Minister undertakes a review under section 294.5 of the [Canadian
  Environmental Protection Act, 1999](/eng/acts/C-15.31).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must, no later than one year after the
  day on which the review is undertaken, cause a report on the review to
  be tabled in each House of Parliament.

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Application of Provincial Schemes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-247042 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[federal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[federal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means land that belongs to Her
    Majesty in right of Canada, or that Her Majesty in right of Canada
    has the power to dispose of, and all waters on and airspace above
    that land. ([territoire domanial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[federal work or undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[federal work or undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work
    or undertaking that is within the legislative authority of
    Parliament, including

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking operated for or in
      connection with navigation and shipping, whether inland or
      maritime, including the operation of ships and transportation by
      ship;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a railway, canal, telegraph or other work or
      undertaking connecting one province with another, or extending
      beyond the limits of a province;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a line of ships connecting a province with any
      other province, or extending beyond the limits of a province;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a ferry between any province and any other
      province or between any province and any country other than
      Canada;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} airports, aircraft and commercial air services;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a broadcast undertaking;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a bank;

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking that, although wholly
      situated within a province, is before or after its completion
      declared by Parliament to be for the general advantage of Canada
      or for the advantage of two or more provinces; and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking outside the exclusive
      legislative authority of the legislatures of the
      provinces. ([entreprises fédérales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reserve and any other land that is set apart
      for the use and benefit of a band under the [Indian
      Act](/eng/acts/I-5), and all waters on and airspace above that
      reserve or land; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} land that is subject to a comprehensive or
      specific claim agreement, or a self-government agreement, between
      the Government of Canada and an Indigenous people of Canada, and
      all waters on and airspace above that land, with respect to which
      title remains with Her Majesty in right of Canada. ([terres
      autochtones]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned by the definition [[aboriginal peoples of
    Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 35(2) of the
    [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/). ([peuples autochtones du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[provincial law]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[provincial law]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an Act of the
    legislature of a province that relates to the control or pricing of
    greenhouse gas emissions and any regulations made under that
    Act. ([texte législatif d'une province]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, by regulation, made on the recommendation of
  the Minister of the Environment, provide that a provincial law applies
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a federal work or undertaking that is located in
    that province;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} federal land or any part of that land that is
    located in that province;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Indigenous land or any part of that land that is
    located in that province;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any part of the internal waters of Canada that is
    located in or contiguous with that province; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any part of the territorial sea of Canada, the
    exclusive economic zone of Canada or the continental shelf of Canada
    that is contiguous with that province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to any provision of a
  provincial law that imposes a tax.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, this section must not be
  interpreted as providing a basis for any claim, by or on behalf of a
  province, in respect of any interest in or legislative jurisdiction
  over any offshore area to which a provincial law is applied under this
  section or the living or non-living resources of that area, or as
  limiting the application of any federal laws.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** The [Statutory
Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does not apply to any instrument made
by a provincial official or body under the authority of a provincial law
that is incorporated by reference in a regulation made under subsection
263(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

**[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
the [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not apply to any fee,
charge or levy that is fixed under a provincial law that is incorporated
by reference in a regulation made under subsection 263(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7)

- **[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  provincial official or body that exercises a power or performs a duty
  or function under a regulation made under subsection 263(1) is not a
  federal board, commission or other tribunal for the purposes of the
  [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review or appeal in provincial courts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise provided by a regulation made under
  subsection 263(1), if a power is conferred or a duty or function is
  imposed by a provincial law that is incorporated by reference in the
  regulation, the exercise of the power or the performance of the duty
  or function is subject to review by, or appeal to, the courts of the
  province in the same manner and to the same extent as if the laws of
  that province applied.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusive economic zone and continental
shelf

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
an individual who exercises a power or performs a duty or function under
a regulation made under subsection 263(1) may do so in the exclusive
economic zone of Canada or in waters above the continental shelf of
Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amounts collected

**[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** Payments collected by a
provincial official or body under a regulation made under subsection
263(1) belong to Her Majesty in right of the province and are not public
money for the purposes of the [Financial Administration
Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for acts and omissions

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** In respect of any act
or omission occurring in the exercise of a power or the performance of a
duty or function under a regulation made under subsection 263(1),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada is entitled to the
  same limits on liability, defences and immunities as those that would
  apply to Her Majesty in right of the province in question when Her
  Majesty in right of that province exercises such a power or performs
  such a duty or function under the law that applies in that province;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person or body exercising the power or performing
  the duty or function is entitled to the same limits on liability,
  defences and immunities as those that would apply to a person or body
  when the person or body exercises such a power or performs such a duty
  or function under the law that applies in that province in question.

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Report to Parliament]{.HTitleText1} {#h-247090 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

**[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** Starting in the year in
which the second anniversary of the day on which this section comes into
force falls and each calendar year after that, the Minister of the
Environment must prepare a report on the administration of this Act and
have a copy of the report tabled in each House of Parliament.
